ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u8	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Gentlemen! I don't think anyone here is questioning the fact that the people of Bieszczady, the citizens, are well protected by nature, and they've known what's going on for years. I'm sure no one wants to teach them that. I also think that the issue of nature conservation in Poland is a topic about which we all know that it arouses great emotions. Most are in favour of nature conservation, but the closer our habitat is to the proposed forms of nature conservation, the more worried about the limitations that follow. It's natural, and I understand that, too. All the more so, I think that it is good that you are bringing this up and asking the question, because citizens have the right to know where certain decisions come from and what they themselves have the opportunity to take part in making or ultimately drawing up a plan. I think that when discussing the plans of Nature 2000, it is worth recalling those issues that illustrate why protection plans are created in Poland, including in Bieszczady. It is worth recalling that together with Poland's accession to the European Union, we have accepted certain obligations in relation to compliance with legal regulations, including those concerning Natura 2000 areas. Secondly, Polish law, and here specifically the Nature Conservation Act, indicates that the Minister is setting up a conservation plan, guided by the need to maintain and restore to the proper conservation status of all natural habitats and species of plants and animals for which the area has been designated. Some of these habitats and species are also found in private areas or land. These plans also refer to spatial development, so it is not so that they are contradictory. They must take into account land-use issues, must take into account the location of possible buildings without damage to protected areas, technical infrastructure, communication infrastructure, tourist infrastructure, education infrastructure, as well as deforestation of forested or unforested areas. The plan includes not only the conditions for maintaining and restoring the proper state of protection, but also - and I think that in the context of this question is This is very important - the conditions for maintaining the integrity of the area and the coherence of the network of areas should be included. Therefore, the exclusion of part of the area, even if it were to be private land, is incompatible with Polish and EU regulations. This could also lead to the degradation of natural habitats or the disappearance of species. Of course, the Regional Environment Director in Rzeszów, responsible for the project created, who also supervises the protection of this area - just like every Director of WDOŚ in Poland - must be aware, and I think he is aware that there are specific expectations, needs and concerns of the local community. It should seek and, as far as I know, seek to reach a compromise and draw up a document which, on the one hand, will be acceptable to the inhabitants and, on the other hand, will ensure the real protection of valuable natural habitats or plant and animal species found there. It's obviously not easy. We have conflicting interests here. Work on the plan started in 2014 and they are still ongoing today. So I think it is difficult to point out the binding shape of the solutions that have been developed, but there is a will on the director's side to bring about this compromise and to correct these social tensions. The point is that if certain things cannot be accomplished according to the expectations of the inhabitants, explain what these limitations result from doing certain tasks. I hope this works. Of course, the Ministry is at the disposal of the residents. These writings also affect us. We are kept informed of all doubts that arise. We are also in contact with the Director of WDOŚ, obliging him to take a particularly sensitive approach to this issue. Thank you.

In response from the Ministry, we heard that Prince Czartoryski, or the Foundation of Princes of Czartoryski, gave up these claims in the agreement described in the notarial act of December 2016, while this agreement concerns the movable property in the palace of the former Sieniawska Order and does not constitute anything concerning the property itself and the possible waiver of claims. That's why [[♪ The bell ♪]] In this respect, we ask the Minister to explain.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u17	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage of Mr Paweł Lewandowski.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u18	Mr. Marshal! Mr. Speaker! High Seym! The historic palace in Sieniawa will remain the property of the State Treasury. Thanks to the purchase by the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage of the collection of Princes of Czartoryski, the palace and park complex in Sieniawa will remain the property of the State Treasury. Purchase transaction of the Czartoryski collection of December 2016, in addition to Leonardo da Vinci's painting, 86 thousand. museum objects and 250,000. library facilities also covered claims of the heirs of the Czartoryski family for real estate, including the historic palace in Sieniawa valued at over PLN 22 million. Media reports on the fact that Czartoryski may now demand reimbursement of the refurbished state property money are false and testify to another manipulation of this medium on the purchase of the Czartoryski collections. The agreement concluded between the State Treasury and the Foundation of Princes of Czartoryski and Adam Karol Czartoryski in December 2016 also covered reprivatization claims to the palace and park complex in Sieniawa. That's how the Treasure works. The States will not bear any costs for the transfer of ownership of the team and shall not be obliged to pay to the heirs of the palace. Here I would like to point out that the contract was concluded with the foundation and with the prince, because not all things belonged to the foundation - some were the direct property of the prince. The decision to purchase the Czartoryski collection, including the park and palace complex in Sieniawa, allowed to avoid the return of this property and thus - the loss of huge public funds, as happened for many other properties recovered recently as part of the reprivatization. On 28 June 2013, the Podkarpackie Voivodeship issued a decision showing that the park and palace complex in Sieniawa was not covered by the decree on agricultural reform of 6 September 1944. Due to the public money transferred to the renovation of the palace, as well as its historical value and the ongoing reprivatization proceedings since the 1990s, on the occasion of the acquisition of the Czartoryski collection in December 2016, the Minister of Culture and National Heritage Piotr Gliński made sure that the palace and park complex in Sieniawa was owned by the State Treasury. In the light of settled case law, which indicates that the reprivatisation claims resulting from the decree for agricultural reform - as administrative claims - cannot be effectively waived, and that such claims cannot be traded, agreements have been concluded between Adam Karol Czartoryski, the Foundation of the Princes of Czartoryski and the Treasury. Under the agreements in the event of reprivatisation, the transfer of ownership of the team to the Treasury or indicated by the State Treasury is a legal person. As a result, the public property of the team is confirmed, and any reprivatisation claims are ultimately extinguished. The decision of the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development on the Sieniawa Palace and Park Team, which maintained the decision of the Podkarpackie Voivodeship, was issued on 5 December 2017, and therefore the condition laid down in the above agreements has been fulfilled. Their implementation is completed, the necessary formalities are to be completed within a few months. In the perpetual books as the owner of the property in Sieniawa the State Treasury is indicated, while the team - without change - is at the disposal of the National Agricultural Support Centre, the former Agricultural Property Agency. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u28	Thank you very much. And please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Leszek Skiba.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u29	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The question about SkOK Śląskie Piekary... This is one of the several cases that we observe, which is related to the fact that the legal situation of the members of the cash register is specific, unique. I will start by thinking about the legal situation of people who are in a relationship with syndicates such as the Silesian Piekary. We also observe the actions of the insolvency syndicate SKOK Ark in relation to the members of the cash register. First of all, it is worth remembering that the syndicate, in accordance with the bankruptcy and resolution laws, shall report to the Commissioner within the time limits set, or at least every 3 months, on its activities and on the accounts in advance. On completion of their activities, the syndicate and its deputy shall submit a final report on their activities and subsequent accounts. The Commissioner's judge has the right to reprimand the Syndicate and his deputy in connection with the negligence of his duties. He can impose a fine. The Commissioner-General may revoke the syndicate if he fails to discharge his duties properly. So we're dealing with a direct authority to respond to the syndicate's actions, which is the Commissioner's judge. This is the most important instrument, one can say, direct impact in a situation where the law is actually being violated. It is also worth remembering that there are provisions on the syndicate's license, which provide for a situation in which the Minister of Justice may revoke the syndicate's licence or suspend the rights deriving from the licence if there are gross infringements of the law when the syndicate's actions are unlawful. It is important that all activities of the syndicate are under the control of the Commissioner's judge and, in the case of licences, the Minister of Justice. This activity is under judicial control and this is a direct response to what is happening in this area. Now I'm going to talk about the role of the Ministry of Finance. Of course, we are analysing these issues in agreement with the Financial Supervision Commission, which also observes this from the point of view of stability in relation to its activities, the Banking Guarantee Fund. Indeed, we are gathering information, we are trying to understand it, and of course we are responding to all these issues. We have no direct impact on the case, because the institution which has a direct translation into the activities of the syndicate is the Commissioner's judge and he can interfere. What we're doing is we're trying to identify actions that can actually be questionable. About the fact that members of the cash register are being called, or rather about people who aren't members of the cash register... We have not met with such situations, therefore this is the first such information for us. What we know and what we're trying to respond to... Because there is indeed a provision based on the Cooperative Law, concerning the fact that if in the statutes of a given cashier it is recorded, the syndicate may call upon a member of that cashier - in the event of bankruptcy or bankruptcy - to pay the second amount, it is regulated by the statute of the cashier. On our part, we observe, we try to analyze whether this is done correctly, we collect information. We know, for example, that on 25 October 2017 a civil court in Dąbrowa Górnicza took the first sentence in the case for payment precisely for this double liability, i.e. supplementing the second member's deposit, from the insolvency syndicate's claim of the Ark SkOK against one of the Ark's cashier's members, and it was a judgment in favour of the syndicate. The verdict is incorrigible, so we don't know exactly what's going to happen, but we see that there's some judicial practice. It is based, of course, on this provision that if the syndicate identifies the members of the cash register, and it is written in the statutes, once again here this condition I will remind you, it can expect and demand from the members of the cash register a second deposit of its share. Whether it's always done correctly, it has to check the court. If there are such cases that it is directed to people who are not members of the cash register, then it is unlawful. But to do so, one should react in such a way that those who are in this relationship have the right to attend a meeting of creditors, the Council of Creditors, and can express their right. This is a very difficult case because bankruptcy and resolution law clearly regulates who can interfere with the activities of the syndicate and who can control and verify these activities. So it is worth remembering this in this context. Of course, the information she has presented to you will be analysed and we will be familiar with it again, so that we can check it out and pass it where it is needed, if there is indeed the irregularities that you have been talking about. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u37	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! According to Art. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 point 1 of the Act of 10 April 2003 on specific rules for the preparation and implementation of investments in public roads of real estate or parts thereof contained in the decision to authorise the implementation of road investment shall become the property of the Treasury in respect of national roads as from the date on which the decision to authorise the implementation of the road investment became final. A separate administrative decision issued by the authority which has issued the decision to authorise the implementation of the road investment shall be determined for the immovable property or parts thereof. The competent authority to issue both the decision to authorise the implementation of the road investment so-called. ZRID, as well as the compensation decision, is in this case the Kuyavian-Pomeranian voivode. The Board of Appeal shall be composed of the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing, who shall be competent in matters relating to property matters and expropriations. Currently, the Minister in charge of government administration is Minister of Investment and Development. For this reason, in my speech, I will only try to limit myself to the competence which is appropriate for the Minister of Infrastructure. Most importantly, the value of each property is determined by an independent asset valuer individually in the form of an estimated operation. According to art. 157 The Real Estate Management Act shall assess the correctness of the calculation of the estimated operation in particular by the established professional organisation of property experts within a period of not more than 2 months from the date of conclusion of the contract for carrying out the valuation. It should also be borne in mind that property is a statutory professional activity. The Real Estate Management Act clarifies the scope of activities, the principles of entitlement and the professional responsibility of property experts. In the light of Art. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 1 This Law requires the value of the property to be determined in accordance with the principles of law and professional standards, with particular care specific to the professional nature of these activities and the principles of professional ethics, following the principle of impartiality in the valuation of the property. The establishment of an estimated operation, regardless of the type of conduct, is always emotional because it de facto determines the value of compensation. It must be borne in mind, however, that it is not the government administration, and in this case the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways, which estimates the value of the property and, consequently, the amount of compensation, while in the absence of any reservation for the respect of the operation, its role is actually limited, one can say, to the payment of the amount indicated in the decision. During the preparation of the S5 investment, the residents of the town of Wiąg indicated that the valuation of the assets was disproportionately low. According to the information held by the Ministry and the General Directorate, meetings were held on the subject of residents with the voivodship, which indicated that owners who had reservations about the valuation had requested a re-evaluation of the fixed values, but only if the compensation decision had not been issued. In conclusion, the right of owners to obtain fair compensation for the acceptance of their property rights is guaranteed by indicating by the legislator the appropriate procedure to determine the amount of compensation. On the other hand, the parties dissatisfied with the decision setting the amount of compensation may, of course, exercise their right to appeal against the decision. According to the information held by the infrastructure department, administrative proceedings for the determination of compensation for the property taken over for the construction of the S5 road, section Nowe Marzy - Dworszko, are still being conducted by the Kujawsko-Pomorskie Voivodeship, and in relation to some of the decisions issued, the voivode has been appealed to the Minister of Investment and Development, which is currently considering them. As long as the compensation process is not completed and the valuers appointed by the voivode perform the valuations, including the repeated valuations, this process cannot be considered to be completed. The Directorate-General, as well as the citizens referred to in the question, is a party to this procedure and expects all the issues at issue to be clarified as soon as possible. However, this does not change the fact that the decision of the ZRID concerning this section is already final. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u46	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The implementation of the tasks related to the EU coordination of family benefits and educational provision was the subject of the activities of the voivodships marshals by the end of 2017. The entry into force of the programme 500+. The number of family cases that had never previously applied for any family benefits under EU rules increased by 60% in relation to only family benefits. In short, the amount of education was encouraged by those who had not benefited from the benefits of EU coordination so far, because they could count on much lower family benefits to benefit from it. In view of the unsatisfactory progress made in the implementation of the case, it was decided to take over on 1 January this year. under the Law amending the Law related to systems of support for families coordination of family benefits and educational provision by voivodship offices. The transfer to voivodships of the cases under consideration concerning the EU's coordination of benefits was so justified that this is a task in the field of government administration, which was only commissioned to local governments. Thanks to the change introduced, the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy has also gained much greater impact on the enforcement of effective implementation of EU social security coordination tasks in this area. On the basis of verified information provided by the voivodship offices as of 5 January this year. The marshals of the voivodships handed over over 222 thousand to the voivodships. Unfinished matters concerning the provision of education and family benefits, both cases where administrative and pending proceedings have not been initiated at all, i.e. cases which have been initiated but have not been completed. The Ministry keeps up-to-date, meticulously, closely monitors progress in the implementation of the cases reported by the marshals while verifying the data received at the same time. This monitoring system consists of several elements. By the 5th day of each month following the month to which the report relates, the voivodship offices shall provide information on applications, cases which have entered the voivodship in the calendar month concerned, as at the last day of the month and on the number of decisions taken. Statistical information on the number of decisions taken in family and educational procedures shall be provided each week. As regards financing issues taken over by the voivodship offices, the funds for its implementation were increased by PLN 5 million compared to the previous year. If the amounts received by the voivodships are insufficient, it will be possible to increase this envelope as required. An efficient organisation of work is crucial in the case of the task taken over. Thus, within the voivodship offices special organisational cells were created dedicated exclusively to handling matters related to the coordination of social security systems, information points were created for the citizens concerned, where everyone can learn about the status of their case. The organisation of work has also been modified by placing greater emphasis on the number of employees responsible for carrying out such proceedings and issuing administrative decisions, which has the effect of speeding up the implementation of cases. The recruitment processes announced in order to supplement or increase staff taken over from marshal offices are currently ending. Ministry staff have conducted substantive training on EU legislation on the coordination of social security systems dedicated in individual newly employed voivodships, but also on the deepening of the knowledge of people who have so far carried out this task under the supervision of marshals. The Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy has developed an improved cooperation procedure at the level of the municipality - a voivode to improve the organisation of work, speeding up the implementation of applications. Staff of municipal bodies were trained in most voivodships. On the basis of the new procedure, all matters relating to the need to supplement the applications of the voivodships shall be dealt with by the competent authorities of the municipalities at the level of the closest applicant. The voivodship offices have taken over from the marshals, as I mentioned, 222 603 cases and until 27 April this year. 31 230 decisions were taken, representing around 14% It's taken care of. At the same time, it appears from the analysis of the number of decisions taken in relation to applications for education that in March the voivodship offices issued 5876 decisions, i.e. 393, i.e. 6.7% , more than marshal offices in the corresponding period of 2017. In April [[♪ The bell ♪]] Voivodship offices spent more than 7 thousand. the decision, i.e. It's over 3,000. decisions more than marshal offices in the same period of 2017. The analysis also shows that in the absence of outstanding cases transmitted by the Marshal's Office, current applications could be carried out faster than during the period during which the Marshals of the Voivodships were responsible for this task. It therefore appears that taking over this task will prove to be a positive solution in the short term for those awaiting family benefits and, once the arrears have been resolved, applications will be dealt with in accordance with the deadlines laid down for such matters, so that the pending families can benefit fully from the benefits due to them in Poland or in another Member State of the European Union, the European Economic Area and Switzerland. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u62	Thank you. And please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health of Mr Marcin Czecha.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u63	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, please. (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Referring to the questions of Mr Jan Kilian and Mr Aleksandr Mr Forczyński on the deficit of certain medicines in the context of illegal export abroad, I would like to explain that the authorities of the State Pharmaceutical Inspection are taking action to safeguard the availability of medicinal products for Polish patients. One such action is the inspection of pharmaceutical wholesalers, whereby wholesale distributors obtain information on exports of medicinal products outside the country. This information shall subsequently be provided to the competent law enforcement authorities. Therefore, this phenomenon is known and we have not yet informed that this issue is resolved. Hence our actions, which I am about to talk about, which are to aim to combat this practice more effectively. In cooperation with voivodship pharmaceutical inspectors, weekly reports are prepared for the Minister of Health on the availability of medicinal products on the territory of the Republic of Poland. The reports are one of the sources on the basis of which the Minister of Health publishes every 2 months lists of medicinal products, medical devices and foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses at risk of lack of availability on the territory of the Republic of Poland. It is worth noting that the 200 items that you have spoken of are actually related to 50.70 medicines, because they are often the same preparations in other doses or in other forms. Hence, it is indeed a list according to the so-called. EANs, i.e. special codes, and therefore in practice this problem has less scope. At the same time, I would like to clarify - and this is an important change - that it is currently being examined, and in fact at the last stage, the amendment of the law on the monitoring system for the carriage of goods by road, which provides for the regulation of a group of medicinal products. Since the entry into force of the Act, the monitoring of carriage will be covered by products included in the list of products at risk of lack of availability mentioned above. So that I would like to stress that this solution will only be geared towards those preparations that are lacking, and this absence is associated with a certain risk to patients and a certain challenge in terms of public health. The amendment of the pharmaceutical law of 2015 introduced an obligation to notify the intention to export or dispose of medicinal products outside the territory of the Republic of Poland against which the Chief Pharmaceutical Inspector objects. While in the second half of 2015 nearly 1,400 applications were submitted to the GIF, the Main Pharmaceutical Inspectorate, which represents nearly 80% of all applications, the number of these applications decreased sharply afterwards. In 2017, only one application was received by the Main Pharmaceutical Inspectorate, which was withdrawn by the party. The experience gained from the more than 2 years of activity of the mechanism of informing the GIF of its intention to export showed that the introduction of the application procedure did not solve the problem of exports of medicinal products outside the territory of the Republic of Poland. We are still dealing with this and it forces us to reach for other mechanisms to deal with it. It has to be said that the system has evolved from the reverse distribution chain to the involvement of often fictional health professionals, under which the so-called. the requirements were purchased with medicinal products and then exported. Therefore, we are also dealing with the very high creativity of drug-exporters abroad. Therefore, the inclusion of medicinal products at risk of non-accessibility in the transport monitoring system is another tool in the fight against illegal drug exports and the way in which the system is sealed. At the time of the entry into force of the Act, any trader carrying products from the listed list must declare this fact in the SENT declaration register referred to by the Member. Access to data in the system has services capable of controlling and controlling vehicles on public roads, i.e. Police, Road Transport Inspection or National Tax Administration bodies. As we know, these services work very effectively in other areas. This means for entrepreneurs trying to illegally export medicinal products outside Poland an increased risk of control and consequently imposing severe sanctions. In addition, the current revision of the Pharmaceutical Law and some other laws provides for clarification and addition of the rules governing the control powers of the State Pharmaceutical Inspection, the revision of criminal rules, i.e. criminal sanctions, the regulation of the obligations of medicinal products traders and of target and intervention imports. The cooperation agreement between the authorities of the State Pharmaceutical Inspection and the National Tax Administration was signed on 28 March this year. This agreement sets out a framework for cooperation in particular in the field of information exchange, so we want this exchange of information to take place even earlier than the law will have its power, which is important for the implementation of the statutory tasks of both bodies. On the basis of the provisions of the Agreement, the Parties shall provide information relevant to the effective prevention and marketing of infringements of the laws governing the manufacture of medicinal products and active substances. It should be stressed that the signed agreement aims to strengthen cooperation between the two institutions. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The National Tax Administration has powers which are important for eliminating illegal drug exports. Through the exchange of information and ongoing cooperation, both institutions can achieve greater efficiency in the fight against the unfair practices of certain entrepreneurs. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u81	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The legislative changes introduced have contributed to a significant reduction in the so-called phenomenon. the grey fuel market, as confirmed by the increase in legal fuel trading, well beyond market trends, and the increase in tax revenue from the impact on the state budget. The changes in the fuel market, as a result of the government's legislative actions, had a positive impact on Polish fuel companies, as well as other entities operating on the Polish market, in accordance with the rules and principles of fair competition. The biggest changes were seen in diesel statistics. Effects of the so-called the fuel package was already visible in the first month of its operation. In August 2016, the consumption of diesel was 1319 thousand. t and compared to consumption in July this year was higher by 22%, i.e. 242 thousand. a. There was also a 26% increase in fuel imports, i.e. more than 58 thousand. t more than in the previous month. On the basis of the data provided by the Minister for Energy, public statistics, after comparing consumption and imports of diesel in August 2015 and August 2016, it was found that consumption was higher by 29% while imports were higher by 235%. The positive impact of legislative changes on the market has also been reflected in annual statistics. The consumption of diesel in 2016 was more than 13 million tons and increased by 15% compared to consumption in 2015 , while imports of diesel in 2016 amounted to 3.3 million tons and increased by more than 212% compared to imports in 2015. This growth trend continued in 2017. Consumption growth has been reported almost 3 million t, which means that it was 21% more than in the previous year, and an increase in imports by more than 1.5 million tons, which means an increase of 47%. As far as the motor gasoline market is concerned, the effects of the fuel package are mainly seen in consumption. Annual data indicate an increase in consumption in August 2016 compared to August 2015 by 12% That's over 48,000. t. The consumption of motor gasoline in 2016 alone amounted to almost 4 million t and was higher than consumption in 2015 by 9%, while the import volume of motor gasoline in 2016 was higher by 33% compared to imports in 2015. As with diesel, the upward trend continued in 2017. There was an 8% increase in consumption and an increase in imports by 39%. Production results of 2017 were in line with the demand trends on the domestic market with the maximum use of the possibilities of Polish fuel companies. These companies used both the domestic and the international market for motor fuels to maximise the production of these fuel species, accompanied by a larger official surplus in the country. The fact that the effective elimination of the phenomenon of the black and grey zones on the fuel market has resulted in almost the entire Polish production of domestic fuel companies being placed in the internal market requires emphasis. Products that could not be placed on the internal market have been sold with a good profit abroad. However, this situation cannot be compared with the period before the introduction of solutions to combat the grey zone, since exports were largely forced by the influx of illegal fuels into the domestic market and were carried out under much worse conditions. In 2017, as compared to 2016, exports decreased by 1/3, thus the decreasing trend started in 2016. It should also be stressed that the biggest decrease in exports was related to diesel, motor gasoline, or fuels, which remained largely in the sphere of interest of illicit traders. Exports of diesel from 2016 decreased by 89% and motor gasoline by 56%, which means that around 1 million m3 of fuels were placed in the country more than a year ago. Increased domestic demand also resulted in an increase in imports, especially diesel - by 45%, and motor gasoline - by 32%. Finally, the share of imports in the domestic diesel market also increased to 33%. In the case of motor gasoline, the share of imports in domestic consumption was around 11%. The trends presented are reflected in the financial results of national concerns. And so: PKN Orlen SA - the unit net profit for 2015 amounted to PLN 1048 million, in 2016 it was PLN 5364 million, in 2017.6102 million PLN. That's an increase of 13.4%. in relation to the previous year. The Lotos SA Group - the unit net profit in 2015 is - PLN 37 million, [[♪ The bell ♪]] in 2016, PLN 1160 million, in 2017, PLN 1419 million. The high profits of the companies translated into a visible increase in tax revenue into the state budget. The revenue to the state budget in 2017 amounted to PLN 50 billion, so it is possible to notice about PLN 5 billion in growth compared to 2016.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u87	Thank you very much. Please, Mr. Jan Widera, Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Sport and Tourism.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u88	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer your questions, I would like to inform you that the Minister of Sport and Tourism, in accordance with the Act of 25 June 2010 on Sport, exercises supervision over Polish sports unions. The Sports Act contains a catalogue of restrictions to which a member of the Management Board of the Polish Sports Association is subject. The amendment of this Sports Act of September 2017 introduced a ban on the function of a member of the management board of the Polish Sports Association by persons who are employees, officers or soldiers of the State Security Authorities during the period from 22 July 1944 to 31 July 1990. According to the Act of 18 October 2006 on the disclosure of State Security Authorities documents from 1944 to 1990 and the content of those documents, the catalogue of persons acting as a public office is in principle limited to persons holding public authority or managing positions in public authorities and managers of positions relating to the management of public assets. With regard to sports clubs most often operating in the form of associations or associations, the provisions of the Law of 7 April 1989 on associations apply more widely. In accordance with its provisions, the supervisory authority shall be the competent territorially senior. It should be noted that the association is a voluntary, self-governing and permanent non-profit-making association which sets out its own objectives, action programmes and organisational structures and adopts internal acts concerning its activities. These provisions implement the constitutional principle of freedom of association of citizens and provide considerable autonomy for associations. The provisions of the Law on Associations do not contain the same as the Act on sports restrictions concerning candidates for the composition of the board, in this case also sports clubs and associations. It should be noted that the introduction of dezubecisation in associations active in the field of sport or tourism, including TKKF, would be a violation of the principle of equal treatment of operators as associations operating in other areas would not be covered by this process. It is important that the financial resources allocated to the implementation of sports projects by sports clubs which are associations are awarded through open and competitive selection of applications, so the position of these entities is quite different from that of Polish sports unions, which exercise their powers in a given sport reserved for them in the provisions of the Sports Act. Having regard to the above legal provisions, in particular the fact that the Minister of Sport and Tourism does not supervise the activities of a number of other than Polish sports associations of entities that may carry out activities related to sport, I would like to say that there are currently no work to cover the catalogue of restrictions of persons acting on the boards of associations, including sports clubs. I answer your question about sports clubs and associations. As I said earlier, all these entities have the right to take part in open competitions, where they are thoroughly checked and verified all the documents presented which are related to the rules so that the club receives funding from the Ministry of Sport and Tourism. There are no restrictions on our part here.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u96	Thank you. Please respond to the deputy Attorney General, Mr. Krzysztof Sierak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u97	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, In answer to a question about the actions of the prosecutor's office against police officers who contributed to the death of Igor Stachowiak, to journalists who revealed what had happened at the police station in Wrocław, and to the family of Igor Stachowiak, I kindly inform you that the prosecutor's office does not conduct any action against journalists or against the family of Mr Stachowiak. As regards the first part of the question, concerning police officers, I inform you that the District Attorney's Office in Poznań conducted proceedings concerning the involuntary manslaughter of the death of Igor Stachowiak on 15 May in Wrocław at the police station in the course of carrying out his duties with him by police officers, i.e. Article 1 is replaced by the following: 155 Penal Code. As far as all the evidence gathered is concerned, a number of activities have been carried out meticulously, as well as what was most important in this procedure, i.e. a number of medical opinions, which were summarised in one comprehensive opinion. On the basis of this comprehensive expert opinion attached to the case, which was the basis for assessing the cause of death of Mr Igor Stachowiak, the experts pointed out that the victim died of cardiopulmonary failure following an episode of excited delirium caused by psychotropic drugs. It was the experts who stated in the opinion that there was no reason to accept that the use of taser by police officers would contribute to the death of Mr Stachowiak. It was the experts in a comprehensive opinion based on all the evidence that the evidence does not indicate that the transport and restraint devices used were performed in a way that could directly lead to the death of Mr Stachowiak. It was also the expert opinion that found, ultimately, that the prosecutor was based on the prosecution's indictment that it could not be proved for sure required in criminal proceedings that the measures of direct coercion by police officers in the form of debilitating grips were in connection with the cause and effect of the victim's death. After assessing all the evidence collected, the prosecutor considered that there was no causal link within the meaning of criminal law between the behaviour of police officers and the death of Igor Stachowiak. On 20 March 2018, an indictment was drawn up against four police officers who were accused of exceeding their rights and abuse of a deprived person, i.e. Article 1 is replaced by the following: 231 § 1 of the Penal Code and Art. 247 § 1 of the Penal Code. The indictment was brought to the District Court for Wrocław-Centrummieście in Wroclaw and finally, in this case, an independent court will state its findings, the legal qualifications adopted and the blame of the accused. By the way, I can only mention - I do not think that I am repealing any secrecy of judicial proceedings - that the results of the first provisions of the court on the applications of attorneys of the defendants concerning the change of legal qualifications of the defendants indicate that the legal qualification of the defendants is, at least at this stage of the proceedings, fully correct. As regards the second part of the question, the one concerning the actions of the prosecutor's office towards journalists who have revealed what has happened at the police station and the actions of Igor Stachowiak's family, I must point out once again that the prosecutor's office does not prosecute journalists and does not prosecute members of the Igor Stachowiak family. In accordance with the principle of legalism, resulting from the Code of Criminal Procedure, the prosecutor was obliged to initiate a preliminary investigation, because the transgression of the article. § 241 1 of the Penal Code is prosecuted from the office. In the course of the proceedings on 24 October 2017, the prosecutor addressed the application to the District Court in Warsaw for the release of journalist Wojciech Bojanowski, author of the aforementioned. a report in the program Superwisier, with journalistic secrecy. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u107	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Mr. Marshal, if you don't mind, I'd like to divide the government side into two parts. High Izbo! I would like to inform you that there are over 200 different types of helicopters currently operating in military service. They are designed for a wide range of impact, transport, rescue, reconnaissance and training tasks. These are Mi-24W, 3-PL Głuszec and Mi-2 combat helicopters in the ZOP version, i.e. Mi-14 and SH-2G, so-called. On-board helicopters. We have transport helicopters: W-3 Falcon, Mi-8 and Mi-17, SAR version helicopters: W-3 Anaconda and Mi-14. There are also training helicopters Mi-2 and SW-4 Puszczyk. These helicopters, depending on the type, have a target operating standard used on average above 70%. Despite the long-term lifetime, a relatively high technical efficiency of around 75% is maintained. Maintaining the efficiency level depends on the industry's capacity and the availability of spare parts. In relation to the most exploited helicopters, work has been started to determine the possibility of extending their target standard of operation, which will allow further use of these helicopters by the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The most difficult situation is currently in maintaining the efficiency of SH-2G helicopters, which despite the use of the target operating standard of 50% they will have to be withdrawn due to the lack of further support by the manufacturer for these helicopters. With regard to the tasks carried out and depending on the planned lifetime of the equipment groups, both renovation and modernisation projects and the acquisition of new helicopters are carried out and planned. As far as the spectrum of tasks is concerned, the following projects are planned. In the case of combat helicopters, a limited upgrade of Mi-24 helicopters and the acquisition of new impact helicopters is planned after 2022, while for the W-3 Głoszec helicopters their further operation is planned. With regard to the SH-2G on-board helicopters in the ZOP version, due to the termination of technical support by the manufacturer, it is planned that these helicopters will be replaced by four new helicopters, acquired under the current procedure to obtain those helicopters with SAR function. As regards multi-purpose transport helicopters, repair of Mi-17, Mi-8, W-3 transport helicopters is planned. This will allow them to continue to operate and ensure that the transport tasks of the Armed Forces are carried out over the next 10 years. For SAR helicopters, modernization of W-3 helicopters to the W-3ARM version of Anaconda is carried out. In addition, SAR tasks are carried out by upgraded Mi-14 helicopters. As far as school helicopters are concerned, there are planned renovations and operating support mainly for all SW-4 Puszczyk helicopters, but also Mi-2. In addition, proceedings are underway to obtain light school helicopters for the Dęblin Higher Air Force School. The total expenditure of PLN 4700 million will be allocated to the above objectives in the technical modernisation plan by 2022, which is worth noting. In summary, it should be stressed that the most urgent task now is to obtain new ZOP helicopters in the background version to replace them planned to withdraw SH-2G helicopters in the coming years. Further decisions on the acquisition of further helicopters of different types in the period after 2022 will be drawn up in the framework of the development programme of the Armed Forces for the years 2017 to 2026. The planned deadline for the completion of the work is subject to the adoption by the Council of Ministers of detailed directions for the reconstruction and technical modernisation of the Armed Forces of the Republic for the years 2017 to 2026. We discussed this at the Parliamentary Committee on National Defence, and I am convinced that the Council of Ministers will be leaning on this issue in May. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u161	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law on collective management of copyright and related rights. I would like to remind you that this submission is adapted to European Union law and is intended to implement the 2014 Directive on collective management of copyright and related rights in the Polish legal order and to grant multi-territorial licences on online rights to musical works in the internal market. The transposed Directive specifically regulates almost all aspects of the activities of collecting societies, management bodies. In the preamble, this complexity of legislation is justified by significant differences in national rules governing the functioning of collecting societies, in particular as regards their transparency and accountability to their members and rightholders. The main objectives of legislation are: to ensure coordination of national rules on the conditions for taking up activities in the field of copyright and related rights management and to lay down requirements to ensure a high level of financial management and management and transparency and reporting. The explanatory memorandum of the government project states that the directive harmonises the principles of collective rights management organisations and related rights, i.e. primarily rights to works, artistic performances, phonograms and videograms - to some extent, the rights to publications and press are also managed collectively. After the first reading of the Government's submission at the meeting of the Sejm, the Sejm sent them to the Committee on Culture and Media for consideration. The Commission shall, in accordance with Article. 70a of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, decided to hold a public hearing on this draft law, which was held on 14 March this year. The hearing was attended by 61 people representing, apart from collective management organisations, especially creative, journalistic and cinematic associations, as well as trade unions of cultural artists, artists, authors, publishers and entities such as the Polish Chamber of Electronic Communications, the Polish Chamber of Books and the Chamber of Press Publishers. Following a public hearing, the committee called for an extraordinary subcommittee to consider the government draft law on collective management of copyright and related rights. The subcommittee considered the bill at its meetings on 20, 21 and 22 March and on 11 and 12 April of this year. The Committee on Culture and Media adopted a subcommittee report with 7 abstentions. During the work of the Sub-Committee and the Commission, a number of legislative, linguistic, clarification and codification amendments and substantive amendments have been introduced in the draft, including in response to comments made during the public hearing. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Culture and Media requests the High Sejm to adopt the draft law annexed to the report. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u163	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law on collective management of copyright and related rights, print No. 2232 and print No. 2491, the report of the Committee on Culture and Media. This law follows a Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union which entered into force in April 2014 and its implementation into Polish law expired in April 2016. Therefore, it is urgent to implement this directive into the Polish legal order. Here some things have already been said by the President on behalf of the committee, so that I will only add that a public hearing took place on 19 March this year, during which the authors made very many valuable comments, proposals, and general opinions and observations concerning, among other things. for greater protection of the rights of Polish artists. Among them were musicians, such as bass guitarist Budki Suffler or one of the Trubadurians, as well as author of legendary text or, perhaps it would be better to say, outstanding autobiography. Next, an extraordinary subcommittee was working closely on the bill, including with a strong government commitment, at five meetings in March and April, and finally at the Committee on Culture and Media. Since matter is as important as and complicated, despite the adoption of extremely numerous amendments in the course of this work, the Law and Justice Club still notes the need to clarify certain entries. Therefore, your marshal, on behalf of the club, I present seven more amendments and their justification. Maybe just a little bit of justification. The first amendment aims to improve the wording of the provision to make it clear, in line with the intention of the projector, that the obligation to make a declaration concerns a candidate to assume the role of the manager of the body responsible for carrying out collective management activities and not a person already actually managing the activities of the organisation. Before the date of taking up that function, and that is when it is to make an appropriate statement, it may only be a candidate for it. The second amendment aims to add to the obligation to make available information also the obligation to make documents available. In this way, the proposed wording of the Article. 49 corresponds to the wording of the article. 50 of the proposed law, explaining potential interpretation doubts as to whether the contract may also contain an obligation to make certain documents available. In practice, such an obligation arises on several occasions from the contract. In the case of the third amendment, there is a clear elimination of the question of interpretation related to the number of arbitrators. Thus, by stating that there are 20 of them, the indication that the committee is composed of a chairman and 20 arbitrators shall be deleted. The chairman of the committee shall be elected from among the arbitrators, although he shall not rule on the individual cases which the committee considers. In addition, the amendment corresponds to the wording of the Article. 67 and Article 67 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning In the case of Amendment 4, the aim is to eliminate the question of interpretation as to the length of the term of office of the arbitration panel appointed in place of the arbitration panel whose term of office has expired. In this way, the provision corresponds to the wording of the Article. 65 of the project, establishing a joint term of office of the Committee on Copyright. In the case of amendment 5, it is intended to add to the existing wording the reference to Article. 75. As a result of the adoption of an amendment to the catalogue of tasks of the chairman of the committee, the objective of assessing the impartiality of the composition will be added when it is appointed. In this way, the provision shall become fully consistent with Article. 75 proposed law. The sixth amendment aims to harmonise the scope of the Article. 80 with the scope of Article. 71, according to which one of the tasks of the chairman of the committee is to transmit to the Minister all decisions terminating the proceedings. Consequently, it is appropriate for the Minister to publish all such decisions in the BIP, all the more so as they concern matters relevant to many users of authors and related rights. And finally the last, seventh amendment. Its purpose is to clarify the subject of the statutory auditor's audit with regard to the financial data contained in the OZZ reports. From the current wording of the proposed article. 91 was not clear to what extent the statutory auditor would be obliged to examine the financial data contained in the basic report and the report on the enforcement of deductions. According to Art. These 91 figures should be based on accounting records. The proposed amendment to the audit report will indicate whether the above requirement, i.e. the use of the data resulting from the accounting records when drawing up the two reports has been met. Marshal, I'm handing over the corrections. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The Law and Justice Club supports the draft law on collective management of copyright and related rights. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u175	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the governmental draft law on collective management of copyright and related rights. The purpose of this project is to implement Union Directive 2014/26/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 February 2016 on collective management of copyright and related rights and the granting of multi-territorial licences on online rights in musical works in the internal market. In view of the financial penalties that threaten us for a 2-year delay in the implementation of this document, it is important that the work on this project is dynamic and efficient. It is worth noting that although the applicable copyright law is often amended in connection with subsequent directives, this is the first act that comprehensively regulates the area of activity of collecting societies. As the Supreme Court points out in its opinion, the subject matter affected by this project is extremely complex and affects the conflicting interests of various influential groups. That is why regulation is so important, and the solutions proposed are so controversial. The project discussed today is very extensive. First of all, however, it should be stressed that by defining new rules for the functioning of collecting societies, the activities of collecting societies will benefit from transparency, which, according to the opinion, and as experience has shown so far, is very desirable. In addition, the provisions in question bring into legal order the definition of a representative organisation, order rules for the representation by collective management organisations of creators and performers who have not concluded collective management agreements, restrict OZZ's freedom to spend funds and strengthen the position of members that the OZZ will have to adopt if the person concerned fulfils the conditions of membership. The draft law discussed today is undoubtedly a desirable, thoughtful and holistic project covering issues related to the activities of collecting societies. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u177	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! You were informed of the significant problems arising from the activities and case law of the Constitutional Tribunal in 2016, which was approved by a majority by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal on 8 November 2017. It is also available on our website. So I will not discuss in detail the issues and statistics contained in it. I will only draw attention to the most important issues in my opinion. The Constitutional Tribunal consists, as you know, of 15 judges. That's the constitution. There was, however, a time in the Court when its President thought otherwise and granted himself an unknown constitution and laws the power to decide who and when is the judge. Until 27 April 2016, the Constitutional Tribunal included: Andrzej Rzepliński - President, Stanisław Biernat - Vice President, Henryk Cioch, Mirosław Granat, Leon Kieres, Lech Morawski, Mariusz Muszyński, Julia Przyłębska, Piotr Pszczółkowski, Małgorzata Pyziak-Szafnicka, Stanisław Rymar, Piotr Tuleja, Sławomira Wronkowska-Jaśkiewicz, Andrzej Wróbel and Marek Zubik. On 28 April 2016, the term of office was opened by Judge Zbigniew Jędrzejewski, elected in place of Judge Mirosław Granat. On 20 December 2016, the term of office was opened by Judge Michał Warciński, elected in place of Judge Andrzej Rzepliński. I will remind you of the history of the recruitment of judges in 2016. The Constitutional Tribunal Act was adopted on 25 June 2015. It was a famous acceleration after President Andrzej Duda's election. On 8 October 2015, the 7th Sejm adopted resolutions on the election of five judges of the Tribunal whose term of office was to begin after the parliamentary elections. On 25 November 2015, the Sejm of the 8th term of office already adopted a resolution on the lack of legal force of the resolutions on the election of five judges of the Constitutional Tribunal of 8 October 2015, as these resolutions were taken in violation of the current procedure. On 2 December 2015, the 8th Sejm adopted a resolution on the election of five judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. On 3 and 9 December 2015, properly elected judges took a vow to the President of the Republic of Poland. On 3 and 9 December, these judges appeared in the Court to take up the duties of judge. President Andrzej Rzepliński did not allow judges to perform their duties, which was an event in the history of the rule of law, because the President of the Constitutional Tribunal granted himself powers unknown both to the Constitution and to the Act. The five resolutions of the Sejm of 25 November 2015 on the determination of the lack of legal power of the resolutions of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland of 8 October 2015 on the selection of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal were decisive for the proper cast of judges in the Court of First Instance. In support of the draft resolutions, it was stated that the selection of the TK judges carried out on 8 October 2015 was not legally effective and binding in connection with irregularities in the selection procedure. After the adoption of these resolutions, there were three unestablished judges in the Court: for the judges Maria Gintowt-Jankowicz, Wojciech Hermeliński and Marek Kotlinowski, as well as the end of the term of two judges: Zbigniew Cieślak - 2 December 2015 and Teresa Liszcz - 8 December 2015. Following the resolutions of 8 October 2015, on 2 December 2015, the Sejm elected five judges of the Tribunal: Henryk Cioch, Lech Morawski, Mariusz Muszyński, Piotr Pszczółkowski and Julia Przyłębska. The President took a vow from the judges elected on 3 December - 4 judges and 9 December - 1 judge respectively. On 3 December 2015, the Constitutional Tribunal ruled in its judgment on the signature K 34/15 on the constitutionality of certain provisions of the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 25 June 2015. One of the provisions assessed was Article. 137 of this bill. This provision was unique to the Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Act of 25 June 2015 on the Constitutional Court, the deadline for the submission of candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal for seats released in 2015. The Court found that Article. 137 of the Constitutional Tribunal Act, in so far as it concerns the judges of the Tribunal whose term of office expires on 6 November 2015, is in line with the Constitution, whereas to the extent that it concerns the judges of the Tribunal whose term of office expires accordingly December 2 and 8th, it's against the constitution. The operative part of the judgment K 34/15 did not concern the persons selected on 8 October 2015. The selection procedure for a judge consists of several stages, and K 34/15 only concerned the first of them, i.e. the legal basis enabling applications to be submitted with candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The procedure initiated on 8 October 2015 was not finalised due to the errors revealed in the selection process, the adoption of resolutions of the Sejm of 25 November 2015 on the finding of a lack of legal force of the resolutions of 8 October 2015 and the selection of judges of the Tribunal on 2 December 2015. The Sejm, when adopting the resolution of 25 November 2015, did not know the judgment, because it had not yet passed, a tribunal with the signature K 34/15, which fell only on 3 December, so it cannot be read as a questioning of that judgment. The selection of judges on 2 December 2015 was merely the application of the provisions concerning the selection of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in view of the need to fill the positions of judges. In its judgment of 3 December 2015, the Court of Justice ruled on the provisions of the Act, but did not assess the way in which the law was implemented, i.e. the act of choice of judges, since the Constitutional Tribunal is a court of law and not a court of fact. In its order of 7 January 2016, the Council adopted Decision (CFSP) 2016/1389 [2] appointing the members The U/15 Court found that the control of the acts of selection of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal was not a matter for its cognitive purpose. The investigation into the constitutionality of the resolutions of 25 October 2015 and 2 December 2015 was terminated. Following the order of U 8/15, the President of the Tribunal included only two judges, i.e. Julia Przyłębska and Piotr Beszczółkowski. The President of the Tribunal, despite the lack of legal basis, did not include other judges who expected to take up their duties as judges. At the same time, he assigned them offices, granted leave of absence, paid remuneration and other benefits resulting from the employment relationship. Following the retirement of President Andrzej Rzepliński, the Act of 13 December 2016 entered into force. Provisions introducing the Law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and the Act on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. On this basis, the President of the Republic of Poland entrusted the duties of the President of the Court of Justice Julia Przyłębska, the judges Henryk Cioch, Moravian and Muszyński to perform the duties of Judge of the Tribunal. The Act of 13 December 2016 required the judge acting as President of the Court to carry out the procedure for presenting candidates for the position of President to the President of the Republic of Poland. The General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal was convened and held on the basis of the provisions of that law, in which 14 Judges took part. One of the judges was on leave, his absence was justified, and he also stated that he did not want to run for President of the Tribunal and therefore his passive electoral right was not violated in any way and that there was no need to change the date of the meeting. On the other hand, I would like to add that when the President of the Constitutional Court of Judge Rzepliński was elected, the situation was similar. There were only 14 judges, one of the judges was absent, his absence was justified. The election of candidates for the President of the Tribunal was made by a vote. Some judges did not vote, which did not affect the validity of the vote in view of the fulfilment of the requirements of the Article. 21 Provisions introducing a law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and a law on the status of judges of the Tribunal. In accordance with the explicit provision of this law, the election act in the form of a vote was also a resolution of the General Assembly of the Constitutional Tribunal in the election of candidates for President, which did not provide for for another separate vote on the resolution on election. Thus, the voting act in accordance with the applicable rules was a resolution and this was the formula. By the way, referring to the above-mentioned procedure when it comes to the election of President Rzepliński, then no resolution was taken and an extract from the minutes was sent to President Komorowski. The President of the Constitutional Tribunal appointed me, Julia Przyłębska, as President of the Court of 21 December 2016, from among the two candidates presented to him in the resolution of the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 2 April 1997 defines the basis for the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. It refers to the law on the organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal and the proceedings before the Tribunal. 2016 was the year of numerous amendments to the laws concerning the tribunal. In the first half of 2016, the Law of 25 June 2015, subsequently amended by the Law of 19 November 2015, by the Law of 22 December 2015, in part by the Law of 28 January 2016, was published on 8 March 2016. The selected provisions of the Act of 25 June 2015 in the original wording were examined by the Constitutional Tribunal in the case of signage. K 34/15, i.e. the judgment of 3 December 2015. At that time, the Court confirmed the constitutionality of the contested provisions relating to the organisation and procedure and, as part of the non-constitutional procedure, the provision concerning the time limit for the submission of candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal was recognised. The December amendment was the subject of a ruling by the Court in the sign case. K 47/15, issued on 9 March 2016. The Court acted here without the provisions of law in force at the time of judgment. The decision was not announced in the Official Journal of the Laws, which meant that it did not cause a change in the legal system. After 9 March 2016, the majority of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal issued decisions on the basis of such provisions of the proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, which would have been in force if they had decided on the signature. K 47/15 had legal effect in the legal system in the form of a derogation from certain legal provisions. At the same time, part of the judges did not agree to the application of non-binding legal provisions at the time of the ruling, as expressed in separate sentences submitted in cases pending in 2016. In particular, the formation of formation of formations, infringement of the rules concerning the order in which cases are dealt with, the time limits for the appointment of hearings and the presentation of positions by the parties to proceedings before the Tribunal and the recognition of cases in secret were raised in accordance with the law. In view of the situation, there has been a change in the rules on the functioning of the Court by means of the Law of 22 July 2016. It represented a return to the 1997 law in force for many years. It introduced only a few major changes, about seven articles, and the remaining dozen were intended only to bring the rules into line with others already existing in the legal system. Certain provisions of the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 22 July 2016 were challenged to the Court of Justice. The Court of Justice found the allegations on 11 August 2016 in the case of letters. K 39/16. The decision was issued on the basis of non-binding legal provisions and was not published in the Official Journal of the Laws. Irregularities concerning the grounds for judgment, in particular as regards the ruling in the composition of an unknown law, have been raised by certain judges in separate sentences. The end of 2016 brought another amendment to the rules governing the organisation and procedure of the tribunal. These provisions are contained in three laws: the Law of 30 November 2016 on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, the Law of 30 November 2016 on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Law of 13 December 2016. The Rules introducing the Law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and the Act on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. As indicated in the explanatory memorandum to the draft law on organisation and procedure before the Constitutional Tribunal, the separation of its subject matter is intended to refer directly to the reference contained in the Constitution. The legislator considered it reasonable to create a separate law on the status of judge of the Constitutional Tribunal as governing matters not falling within the category of organisation or in the category of proceedings before the tribunal referred to in Article. 197 constitutions. Separate treatment of the implementing rules is justified by the need to ensure consistency and transparency of the new rules. In 2016, the Constitutional Tribunal also operated on the basis of internal rules. On 15 September 2016, the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal adopted a resolution on the adoption of the Statute of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal. Three judges of the Tribunal questioned the legality of the composition of the General Assembly. In 2016, the President of the Tribunal issued five regulations governing the functioning of the Tribunal. The powers of the Constitutional Tribunal include: ruling within the scope of the designated constitution on the hierarchical compatibility of rules, legal standards of normative acts in proceedings initiated by applications, constitutional complaints or legal questions; ruling in matters of conformity with the Constitution of the objectives or activities of political parties; resolving disputes of competence between central constitutional authorities of the State and establishing a transitional obstacle to the exercise of office by the President of the Republic of Poland and entrusting the Marshal of the Sejm with the temporary performance of the duties of President. The hierarchical control may be carried out in a preventive or follow-up control mode. Only the President of the Republic of Poland has competence to initiate preventive control. The subject of this audit is a bill submitted to the President for signature or presented to him for ratification of an international agreement. In 2016, the Court considered two cases initiated by the President in 2015 under preventive control. In 2016, the Court used its basic competence as [[♪ The bell ♪]] ruling on the compatibility of certain normative acts, standards and provisions with the higher-ranking acts referred to in the Constitution. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u219	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on European Union Affairs, I have the honour to present the committee's report on the Commission's draft resolution on the future shape of cohesion policy in the European Union after 2020. The draft resolution is a committee initiative adopted by the Commission for European Union Affairs on 22 March and aims to be heard by the Sejm in a debate in the European Union on the future shape of cohesion policy after 2020. Such resolutions are often adopted by the Polish Parliament also in order to strengthen the voice of the Polish government on the international stage. On 8 February, for example, the Polish Parliament adopted a resolution on negotiating the rules governing the implementation of the common agricultural policy of the European Union after 2020. The draft resolution contained in print 2427 was addressed by the Marshal on 11 April this year. to the Committee on European Union Affairs for first reading, and the committee carried out its first reading and discussed the draft resolution at its meeting on 8 May. In the proposed text of the draft resolution, we stress that maintaining cohesion policy is crucial for the effective implementation of the Community objectives. We note that cohesion policy measures should form an important part of the EU budget and that it should cover the geographical scope of all regions, support the regions with the lowest level of development, calling for the maintenance of GDP per capita as a basic indicator of allocation of funds. In addition, in the resolution, we propose changes to the functioning of cohesion policy: strengthening the thematic concentration mechanism while maintaining a wide range of cohesion policy interventions, as action must be adapted to identified deficits and territorial potentials; changing the logic of the cohesion policy system to support structural reforms and building on positive incentives for structural change; harmonising the implementation of cohesion policy funds and maintaining the current structure of cohesion policy funds, i.e. including the European Regional Development Fund, the European Social Fund and the Cohesion Fund; strengthening the territorial approach through a wider use of territorial instruments, as well as simplifying and making them more flexible, reshaping the multi-level model of shared management so that its main axes are mutual trust and a focus on achieving results so that more competences can be transferred to the national and regional level for implementation, including audit and control; simplifying the use of financial instruments. At the same time, in the resolution, due to the close link between cohesion policy and the efficiency of the whole economic governance system, we are proposing changes to this system so that the recommendations to the Member States are multi-annual and encourage structural reforms at EU and Member State level, and we are calling for a stronger dialogue between the Member States and the Commission so that countries can fully identify with these recommendations. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The content of the proposed resolution expresses the will of the Polish Parliament to preserve the importance and role of cohesion policy in the next period of funding of the European Union. This will is described in a coherent, substantive, politically accentless language, which, I hope, will make this resolution adopted by the High House unanimously and thus strengthen Poland's voice in the EU arena and will give stronger support to the Polish government in the beginning of difficult negotiations. With this in mind, that the Polish Parliament should try to speak with one voice, especially in a matter that is so important for Poland and Poles, which is the future of cohesion policy, I propose that the High Sejm adopt the resolution submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u221	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister!
