Packages of specific regulations for these sectoral initiatives, including cohesion policy, are to be published on 29 May. The proposals are very general in nature, they define the proposed amount of resources and their allocation to specific policies, but do not contain specific solutions, e.g. information on the so-called national envelopes. The proposals put forward do not prejudge anything at this stage and will have to be adopted unanimously by all Member States. In February this year, Prime Minister Morawiecki presented a rational vision of the post-2020 budget. Some of the new objectives of the European Union, particularly in terms of security, migration and external borders, obviously require financial support. However, this cannot be done by simple shifts at the expense of Central Europe. On the last straight before the European Commission presented the draft budget, Poland managed to defend a significant part of the region's interests and to halt the most radical proposals for cuts, the most radical proposals for changes, proposals for a real revolutionisation of the EU budget. On the other hand, this proposal is still a very imperfect document, which merely sets out the prospect of an agreement, but is still far from that agreement. And this is just the beginning of a very difficult and nervous process of negotiating a multi-annual budget in new political circumstances in the European Union. Therefore, the involvement of the Sejm is needed at this stage. We will have two resolutions, one on the common agricultural policy, the other on the cohesion policy. In the course of negotiations, further resolutions of the Sejm are possible, we do not close the road. In order to assess the European Commission's proposals in general, the overall political context in the Union needs to be taken into account. We have brexit, so the reduction in revenue to the Union budget in recent years was EUR 10 The North countries are under even greater pressure for austerity, often from populist forces and the opening up of anti-European forces. The countries of the South are still in a crisis situation when it comes to unemployment. Central Europe on this map is at the forefront of political stability and economic growth. For these reasons, this will be the most difficult budget negotiations in history. Poland, Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, is one of the key players at the table of budget negotiations. The increase in the importance of Poland was also underlined yesterday by Pat Cox, President of the European Parliament from the time of accession. Everyone in Europe, including in the European Commission, is aware that without agreement with Poland, a multi-annual budget cannot be adopted. In the budgetary debate, Poland is one of the most active countries in the European Union, together with a broad coalition of countries for more than 2 years, we have presented well-founded demands in all areas of the budget. Although the European Commission has proposed a reduction in the cohesion policy budget - which will still be negotiated - Poland will still remain the biggest beneficiary of this policy. The first success is already, cohesion policy has been recognised as an important pillar of the European Union's action. At the same time, as parliamentarians in this House, we should at least all represent together the interests of Poland and Poles. It is clear that these new criteria will not work in favour of our country, for example because more than 2.5 million young Poles who have left because of unemployment will probably not be included in the number of unemployed. I think the best proposal from Poland is to propose that the allocation criterion be the average remuneration achieved in a given country. It is the level of earnings that is crucial for the quality of life of citizens. Poland has been at the gray end for years in terms of earnings. According to Eurostat data provided in March this year. Poles together with Bulgaria and Romania are on the podium of countries where workers receive the lowest wage. Poles earn an average of about PLN 27 per hour, and for the Union as a whole this amount amounts to almost PLN 100. For example, in Denmark it is 164 PLN, in Belgium - 160 PLN. I believe that it would be in line with the idea of solidarity and justice, which so far remains in the area of declarations, to adopt as a wage level indicator. In view of the upcoming new EU financial perspective, it is important that we do not put ourselves in the position of a poor relative accepting a donation, but a strong, knowledgeable country. It should therefore be a priority for the government to maintain the value of GDP per capita as the only indicator of the distribution of cohesion policy funds or to supplement it with an average wage. The European Union is a union of equal states, and we have seen for years that the voice of stronger, solidarity-based states has prevailed. The old Union. The Europe of many speeds, and thus the very diverse income of citizens, is a denial of the idea of integration. Let us modify, change cohesion policy, but in a way that will improve it. It is important to harmonise the rules for implementing the Funds, reduce administrative burdens and simplify cohesion policy procedures. These are important issues not only for Poland, but also for other countries where we will probably find allies of our demands. In conclusion, I would like to support the resolution prepared and call on the government not to give in to the pressure to extend the criteria for the allocation of funds, and if so, the main one should be the amount of the average remuneration received in the country concerned. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u333	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen! The first reading of the draft act on Central Communication Port, print No 2473, took place on 8 May 2018 at a meeting in the Sejm. On 9 May 2018, the Committee on Infrastructure, established for the substantive examination of the government draft law, reworked the draft law article after the article. The aim of the bill is to create a legal and organisational framework for the implementation of investments in the form of the Central Communication Port, broadly understood accompanying investments and creating a transport system based on its central location and other tasks, including investment, as well as revitalizations, contained in a comprehensive programming and strategy document to be developed under this law. The proposed regulations are designed to provide specific legal solutions to regulate and streamline the process of efficient preparation and implementation of the overall investment and development objectives. With regard to the draft law, two substantive amendments were adopted at the meeting and 36 legislative and editorial amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau were adopted. These amendments are intended to clarify certain provisions in the draft procedure. The first substantive amendment concerned the deletion of the article. 5 point 6 of the draft law on the repetition of the regulation already contained in Article. 27. The second substantive amendment concerned clarification in Article. Article 34 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 draft law of the concept of an instance of renewable energy source. The Commission requests the adoption of a draft law. On my side, that's all. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

As I said at the first reading, this is a very attractive alternative in one case: when there is no national carrier that can establish at the airport serving Warsaw hub, because air traffic - I'm sorry if I say obvious things, but sometimes you have to - can be divided into two types: either we have a traffic that moves from point to point, point to point, and this is a traffic that is currently being carried out by regional ports, which for this reason are in no way competitive towards the Central Communication Port, or we have hub traffic, with the hub being set up by the airline, and hence the implementation of the Central Communication Port is linked to the existence of a network carrier that would like to create a base in the Central Communication Port. On the subject, the hub traffic is that we bring passengers from different regions, sometimes from different countries, as is the case today. German hubs, Hubs in the Middle East or Hubs in the Far East, in one place, and then we transport them either around the world to areas that we are unable to take off from regional ports, because the belts that are needed are too long for operations to be carried out, or the directions would be inefficient if we considered only passengers from one region. That's what the hubs are for, so we can make the air traffic profitable. In addition, of course, the hub passengers make the best money. The best revenues for airports from passenger transport are derived from the carriage of passengers switching. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u586	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In March 2018, he celebrated his 70th birthday, Fr. Kazimierz Bednarski, one of the most outstanding contemporary Kosvalinian - for 38 years the parish priest of St. The Holy Spirit in Koszalin, co-organizer of John Paul II's visit to Koszalin in 1991, from which began the fourth pilgrimage of the Pope to his homeland, and the first to free Poland. In 1980 Fr. Kazimierz Bednarski became the parish priest of the newly established parish located among the complex of blocks in the north of Koszalina. After a few years there was a temple surrounded by parish buildings. The Church quickly became a marina for those who, living in atheistic reality, had not yet known another world of values and morality, had not yet come to know God and felt the heirs of the great Christian culture from which Europe and the whole civilized world grew up. The tower of the church, like a lantern among the grey blocks, attracted all those who were revived by the breeze of freedom. An independent art gallery was created here, a printing press not available at that time in the country patriotic literature, and the church and its surroundings became the only national shrine in Koszalin with sculptures, plaques commemorating Katyń, Volhynia, Monte Cassino, Boy Scouts of Independent, Cursed Soldiers. At the parish in the early 1990s, one of the first Catholic schools in Poland was established, known today for educational and teaching achievements. Today the parish of St. The spirit in Koszalin is known for many social initiatives, among others. from the family festivals, annual festivals, meetings with folklore, meetings with outstanding people organized by the Catholic Intelligence Club, media initiatives undertaken by secular Catholics, sobriety activities - the annual national sobriety pilgrimage to the Chełmska Mountain, library activities and dissemination of reading. For several years the parish has aspired to the role of the sanctuary of St. John Paul II calling for the Holy Spirit. The title refers to the famous words: Let your Spirit descend! Let your Spirit descend and restore the face of the earth! This land! - said by John Paul II on pl. Winnings in Warsaw during the first pilgrimage to Poland in 1979 and repeated in 1991 at the threshold of Polish independence at the airport in Koszalin. Fr. Kazimierz Bednarski is a doctor of Bible teachings, educator, co-founder and director of the Catholic school. St. John Paul II. He was the first priest in Poland to receive the award. Fr. Bolesław Domanski granted for organic work for Poland. It comes from the parish in Nowa Brzeźnica near Radomska, radiant with the fame of Jan Długosz. 9 May 1976 at the hands of Bishop B.C. Ignacy Jeja was ordained priestly in Białogard. In the jubilee year 2000, the Catholic Information Agency granted him the title of Pastor of the Year 2000. High Izbo! The work of one man who changed the city and played a significant historical role in the change of the turn of two centuries is so impressive that it is difficult to put them in words in a short speech. I think I will receive the approval of the High House to convey from here, from the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, thanks and words of appreciation to the outstanding Koshalin priest, an extraordinary man, Fr. Kazimierz Bednarski. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u587	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Jarosław Sachajko, Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-10-sejm-62-3.u588	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Today, actually yesterday, because it's already hours. 0.20, we've been investigating the Biocomponents and Biofuels Act. This innocent name covers a lot of costs for all citizens, because it says in this bill that we will add another tax to each litre of fuel - 10 gr. What does this 10 gr mean? 2.5 billion a year out of all of us' pockets. This is another increase, we can say tax, not only about the money that will be taken from our pockets, i.e. the 2.5 billion PLN, but it is also an increase in the cost of food, actually all services. This is also a significant increase in the costs of farmers who use fuel in their machines. During this meeting, Marshal Sejm behaved outrageously, took the floor of one of our Members, who wanted to show, visually rearrange the fact that 53% of the fuel costs we pay at petrol stations are taxes. All the confusion has led to the fact that during the vote I was wrong and when we voted on the request made by Kukiz15 to reject the project at first reading, I voted against the proposal. This is an obvious mistake. I find this bill very harmful. It's taking money out of our pockets, and politicians should be looking for it at their own place. On this occasion, I would like to remind you that political parties are taking huge party subsidies, e.g. Law and Justice is 50,000. every day for party activities, whether it's Monday, Saturday, Sunday or New Year's Eve. If we want to save money and want to look for money, political parties should start looking for money in their own pockets, not further in citizens' pockets. Thank you. [[Applause]]
